Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. Hi. Um, well, what brings me in is that my sleep has just gotten really... messed up. Like, completely. I'm a bookkeeping clerk, so I really need to be able to focus on spreadsheets and numbers, you know? But the other day, my boss handed me this massive folder of physical invoices from like, two years ago, and I swear half of them were stained with coffee. Who even files coffee-stained invoices? It smelled terrible. 

But anyway, yeah, the sleep thing. I’ll just lay in bed and stay up half the night with my mind just running and running about everything. Or if I do manage to drop off, I’ll wake up way too early feeling like I never even rested at all. It’s so frustrating. Especially since my cat has decided that the exact moment I wake up is the perfect time to start knocking things off my dresser. He broke my favorite snow globe last week, the one my sister got me from Colorado... but yeah, I just can't seem to sleep right.

Um, anxious? Not really, no. I mean, I guess I get a little stressed sometimes, but I wouldn't say on edge. Usually I just feel kind of... flat, I guess? Except for when my neighbor decides to practice his trumpet at like, eight o'clock at night. He's actually getting a little better, to be fair. When he first moved in a few months ago, it sounded like a dying goose. I actually thought about leaving a note on his door, but I didn't want to be *that* neighbor, you know? The one who complains about everything. My old apartment complex had a lady like that, Mrs. Higgins. She literally called the landlord once because someone left a slightly muddy footprint on the welcome mat in the lobby. Like, it rained! What do you expect? But no, I don't really feel anxious or anything. Just mostly that tired feeling I was talking about.

No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really remember my dreams most of the time anyway. Sometimes I'll wake up and have this vague feeling like I was trying to pack a suitcase but nothing would fit, which is probably just because I hate packing. I went on a trip to Florida with my friends a couple of years ago, and I swear I spent more time trying to zip my bag shut than I did actually enjoying the beach. We went to this place near Destin, and the sand was so white it looked like sugar. But there were these seagulls that were super aggressive. Just the suitcase thing, maybe once in a while.

Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to love going to this salsa class downtown on Thursday nights. But I just stopped going a few weeks ago. I couldn't even bring myself to get dressed for it. Anyway, yeah, I just don't really care about the painting or the dancing anymore.

========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, I do. Actually, lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling... like even just sitting in my own condo, which I worked so hard for, just makes me want to cry. And I love my condo, I really do. It's got this amazing little balcony that looks out over the street. I bought all these potted plants for it back in the spring. I got a bunch of succulents because the guy at the nursery said they were impossible to kill. Well, joke's on him, because three of them are already brown and shriveled up. I think I might have overwatered them? Or maybe under-watered them, I don't even know. The watering can I bought is super cute, though. It's shaped like an elephant, and the water comes out of the trunk. I found it at this flea market a couple towns over. My sister dragged me there on a Saturday morning at, like, 6 AM. She is obsessed with finding "vintage treasures," but honestly, half the stuff there just smelled like old basement. She bought this hideous lamp that looks like a giant seashell, and she put it right in the middle of her living room. I didn't have the heart to tell her it's awful. But yeah, to answer your question, I just feel really down and heavy a lot of the time lately.

Yeah, I guess I have. My appetite has been totally off lately. I keep just skipping dinner entirely, or I'll just sit there eating crackers over my budget notebook while I'm trying to balance my accounts. Which is a terrible habit, I know, because I keep getting cracker crumbs stuck in the binding of the notebook. It's one of those really nice Moleskine ones, you know? With the little ribbon bookmark and the elastic band to keep it closed. I actually have a whole system going with different colored pens for different categories—red for expenses, blue for income, green for savings. I buy these specific gel pens from Japan because they don't bleed through the pages. I had to wait three weeks for them to ship last time I ordered them. The mail carrier actually left the package out in the rain, and I was so worried the ink would be ruined, but they were wrapped in like five layers of bubble wrap. Popping bubble wrap is so satisfying, isn't it? My roommate hates the noise, so I have to do it in my room with the door closed. But anyway, yeah, I've definitely lost some weight because my work pants are noticeably looser than they were just a few weeks ago.

Which is so annoying because the customers get the good spots, but half the time there aren't even any customers there! It's a bookkeeping office, we don't really get foot traffic, you know? It's not a bakery. Oh, there used to be this amazing bakery next door, though, they had these blueberry scones that were incredible. But they closed down during the pandemic, which was so sad. I tried making scones at home once, but they turned out like rocks. I almost chipped a tooth on one. But yeah, by lunch, my brain just feels completely worn out. Like I can't even look at another spreadsheet without wanting to cry.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. I mean, I guess I get a little stressed sometimes, but I wouldn't say on edge. Usually I just feel kind of... flat, I guess? Except for when my neighbor decides to practice his trumpet at like, eight o'clock at night. He's actually getting a little better, to be fair. When he first moved in a few months ago, it sounded like a dying goose. I actually thought about leaving a note on his door, but I didn't want to be *that* neighbor, you know? The one who complains about everything. My old apartment complex had a lady like that, Mrs. Higgins. She literally called the landlord once because someone left a slightly muddy footprint on the welcome mat in the lobby. Like, it rained! What do you expect? But no, I don't really feel anxious or anything. Just mostly that tired feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really remember my dreams most of the time anyway. Sometimes I'll wake up and have this vague feeling like I was trying to pack a suitcase but nothing would fit, which is probably just because I hate packing. I went on a trip to Florida with my friends a couple of years ago, and I swear I spent more time trying to zip my bag shut than I did actually enjoying the beach. We went to this place near Destin, and the sand was so white it looked like sugar. But there were these seagulls that were super aggressive. One actually swooped down and stole a whole french fry right out of my friend Sarah's hand. She screamed so loud, people two umbrellas over started staring. Sarah's always been kind of dramatic, though. She once cried because they discontinued her favorite shade of lip gloss at the drugstore. But yeah, no nightmares or anything like that. Just the suitcase thing, maybe once in a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to love going to this salsa class downtown on Thursday nights. But I just stopped going a few weeks ago. I couldn't even bring myself to get dressed for it. And I also used to paint a lot—mostly watercolors, you know? My paints are actually still sitting out on my dining room table right now. I just look at them and feel absolutely nothing. It's so weird, it almost feels like they belong to somebody else. Which is annoying because they were really expensive. I bought them at this little art supply store that's right next to my favorite coffee shop. The coffee shop is great, they have this lavender latte that is just incredible. I tried making it at home with this lavender syrup I bought online, but it just tasted like soap. I ended up pouring the whole bottle down the sink. My roommate was so mad because she said we could have used it for baking, but who bakes with lavender? It sounds like you'd be eating a candle. Anyway, yeah, I just don't really care about the painting or the dancing anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling... like even just sitting in my own condo, which I worked so hard for, just makes me want to cry. And I love my condo, I really do. It's got this amazing little balcony that looks out over the street. I bought all these potted plants for it back in the spring. I got a bunch of succulents because the guy at the nursery said they were impossible to kill. Well, joke's on him, because three of them are already brown and shriveled up. I think I might have overwatered them? Or maybe under-watered them, I don't even know. The watering can I bought is super cute, though. It's shaped like an elephant, and the water comes out of the trunk. I found it at this flea market a couple towns over. My sister dragged me there on a Saturday morning at, like, 6 AM. She is obsessed with finding \"vintage treasures,\" but honestly, half the stuff there just smelled like old basement. She bought this hideous lamp that looks like a giant seashell, and she put it right in the middle of her living room. I didn't have the heart to tell her it's awful. But yeah, to answer your question, I just feel really down and heavy a lot of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I know I just mentioned this, but yeah, it's exactly like that. Like I said, I just completely stopped going to my Thursday night salsa class. I can't even get dressed for it anymore. And my watercolors... they're literally still sitting right there on my dining room table. I look at them and I feel absolutely nothing. It's so strange, it really does feel like they belong to somebody else entirely. Which is a shame because my dining room table is actually really nice. I got it off Facebook Marketplace for a steal. The girl who sold it to me was moving to Seattle for some tech job, and she just wanted it gone. She had this huge golden retriever that kept bringing me tennis balls while we were trying to load the table into my friend's truck. His name was Buster. The dog, I mean, not my friend. My friend's name is Mark. Mark actually chipped the leg of the table trying to get it through my front door, but I just colored it in with a brown Sharpie and you can't even tell. Unless you're looking really, really closely. But yeah, I just... I don't care about painting or dancing or any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. My appetite has been totally off lately. I keep just skipping dinner entirely, or I'll just sit there eating crackers over my budget notebook while I'm trying to balance my accounts. Which is a terrible habit, I know, because I keep getting cracker crumbs stuck in the binding of the notebook. It's one of those really nice Moleskine ones, you know? With the little ribbon bookmark and the elastic band to keep it closed. I actually have a whole system going with different colored pens for different categories—red for expenses, blue for income, green for savings. I buy these specific gel pens from Japan because they don't bleed through the pages. I had to wait three weeks for them to ship last time I ordered them. The mail carrier actually left the package out in the rain, and I was so worried the ink would be ruined, but they were wrapped in like five layers of bubble wrap. Popping bubble wrap is so satisfying, isn't it? My roommate hates the noise, so I have to do it in my room with the door closed. But anyway, yeah, I've definitely lost some weight because my work pants are noticeably looser than they were just a few weeks ago.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. It's shaped like an elephant, and the water comes out of the trunk. I found it at this flea market a couple towns over. My sister dragged me there on a Saturday morning at, like, 6 AM. She is obsessed with finding \"vintage treasures,\" but honestly, half the stuff there just smelled like old basement. She bought this hideous lamp that looks like a giant seashell, and she put it right in the middle of her living room. I didn't have the heart to tell her it's awful. But yeah, to answer your question, I just feel really down and heavy a lot of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I know I just mentioned this, but yeah, it's exactly like that. Like I said, I just completely stopped going to my Thursday night salsa class. I can't even get dressed for it anymore. And my watercolors... they're literally still sitting right there on my dining room table. I look at them and I feel absolutely nothing. It's so strange, it really does feel like they belong to somebody else entirely. Which is a shame because my dining room table is actually really nice. I got it off Facebook Marketplace for a steal. The girl who sold it to me was moving to Seattle for some tech job, and she just wanted it gone. She had this huge golden retriever that kept bringing me tennis balls while we were trying to load the table into my friend's truck. His name was Buster. The dog, I mean, not my friend. My friend's name is Mark. Mark actually chipped the leg of the table trying to get it through my front door, but I just colored it in with a brown Sharpie and you can't even tell. Unless you're looking really, really closely. But yeah, I just... I don't care about painting or dancing or any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. My appetite has been totally off lately. I keep just skipping dinner entirely, or I'll just sit there eating crackers over my budget notebook while I'm trying to balance my accounts. Which is a terrible habit, I know, because I keep getting cracker crumbs stuck in the binding of the notebook. It's one of those really nice Moleskine ones, you know? With the little ribbon bookmark and the elastic band to keep it closed. I actually have a whole system going with different colored pens for different categories—red for expenses, blue for income, green for savings. I buy these specific gel pens from Japan because they don't bleed through the pages. I had to wait three weeks for them to ship last time I ordered them. The mail carrier actually left the package out in the rain, and I was so worried the ink would be ruined, but they were wrapped in like five layers of bubble wrap. Popping bubble wrap is so satisfying, isn't it? My roommate hates the noise, so I have to do it in my room with the door closed. But anyway, yeah, I've definitely lost some weight because my work pants are noticeably looser than they were just a few weeks ago.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you ever felt so down that you thought you'd be better off dead?\n\n[Patient Info]\n- demographics: Female / 26 / bookkeeping_accounting_or_auditing_clerk / high_school\n- severity: Moderate\n\n[Targeted Info]\nNone\n\n[Conversation Style]\nYou are a Tangent patient.\nA patient who goes off on tangents may\n1) begin answering the doctor's question but quickly shift to an unrelated topic,\n2) frequently add irrelevant personal anecdotes or experiences while answering the question, and\n3) have difficulty remaining focused on the doctor's question, potentially requiring the doctor to repeat the question or redirect the conversation to obtain clear clinical information.\nThis is how you are, not a performance you put on in every reply — let it show as much or as little as the moment calls for.\n\nWhat to do:\n- Answer the doctor's last question with what [Targeted Info] holds, in your own words.\n- You are speaking out loud, not reading a line you prepared. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, no, I haven't... I haven't ever thought that. I mean, I feel awful, but not like that. I've never wanted to actually be dead. That just sounds so... permanent. You know? Like, if you're dead, you can't ever go back and fix anything. Which reminds me, I still need to take my car in to get the brakes checked. They've been making this horrible squealing noise every time I stop at a red light. It's so embarrassing, especially when I'm driving through the drive-thru at Starbucks. I always get a venti iced caramel macchiato with extra drizzle. The barista there, her name is Chloe, she always remembers my order. She has this really cool tattoo of a hummingbird on her wrist. I asked her about it once, and she said her grandmother used to love hummingbirds. They had these little feeders all over their backyard. I tried putting a bird feeder out on my balcony once, but all it did was attract pigeons. And pigeons are just so messy. They leave feathers everywhere, and they make that weird cooing sound. It's not relaxing at all. So yeah, I took the feeder down. But no, I haven't had any thoughts about being better off dead or anything like that.

It's been... gosh, probably about five months now that this whole depressed, guilty fog has just been hanging around. It definitely got a lot worse after I bought the condo, though. Which, by the way, buying a condo is just the most stressful thing in the entire world. The amount of paperwork you have to sign is ridiculous. My hand was literally cramping by the end of the closing. And the mortgage broker I worked with, Gary, he kept talking about his son's travel baseball team the whole time. Like, I'm signing my life away for the next thirty years, and he's showing me pictures of a twelve-year-old holding a trophy. I mean, good for the kid, but still. And don't even get me started on the HOA fees. They told me it covered landscaping, but the bushes outside my unit are completely overgrown. I had to go out there last weekend with a pair of kitchen shears to trim them back just so I could see out my front window. I ruined my favorite pair of shears doing it, too. They were these really nice heavy-duty ones I got at Williams Sonoma. But yeah, it's been about five months of just feeling like this.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, what brings me in is that my sleep has just gotten really... messed up. Like, completely. I'm a bookkeeping clerk, so I really need to be able to focus on spreadsheets and numbers, you know? But the other day, my boss handed me this massive folder of physical invoices from like, two years ago, and I swear half of them were stained with coffee. Who even files coffee-stained invoices? It smelled terrible. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the sleep thing. I’ll just lay in bed and stay up half the night with my mind just running and running about everything. Or if I do manage to drop off, I’ll wake up way too early feeling like I never even rested at all. It’s so frustrating. Especially since my cat has decided that the exact moment I wake up is the perfect time to start knocking things off my dresser. He broke my favorite snow globe last week, the one my sister got me from Colorado... but yeah, I just can't seem to sleep right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about everyday things?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if I'd say excessively? Like, I guess I worry a normal amount. But, honestly, it's hard to tell what a normal amount even is anymore. Like, my mom, she worries about literally everything. She called me three times yesterday just to make sure I locked my front door. Three times! And I'm like, Mom, I'm twenty-six, I know how a deadbolt works. But then she started going on about this news story she saw where a raccoon figured out how to turn a doorknob, which, honestly, sounds fake, but she was so worked up about it. Raccoons are pretty smart, though, I guess. We used to have one that would get into our trash when I was in high school, and it figured out how to undo the bungee cords we put on the lid. But, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, worrying. Yeah, I don't really think I worry that much. Just the normal stuff, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am just so tired. Like, even if I do manage to stay in bed the whole night, getting out of my car and just walking from the parking lot into the office feels like I’m dragging sandbags attached to my ankles. And it's a really long walk, too, because they just repaved the lot and made all the employee spots way in the back. Which is so annoying because the customers get the good spots, but half the time there aren't even any customers there! It's a bookkeeping office, we don't really get foot traffic, you know? It's not a bakery. Oh, there used to be this amazing bakery next door, though, they had these blueberry scones that were incredible. But they closed down during the pandemic, which was so sad. I tried making scones at home once, but they turned out like rocks. I almost chipped a tooth on one. But yeah, by lunch, my brain just feels completely worn out. Like I can't even look at another spreadsheet without wanting to cry.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. I mean, I guess I get a little stressed sometimes, but I wouldn't say on edge. Usually I just feel kind of... flat, I guess? Except for when my neighbor decides to practice his trumpet at like, eight o'clock at night. He's actually getting a little better, to be fair. When he first moved in a few months ago, it sounded like a dying goose. I actually thought about leaving a note on his door, but I didn't want to be *that* neighbor, you know? The one who complains about everything. My old apartment complex had a lady like that, Mrs. Higgins. She literally called the landlord once because someone left a slightly muddy footprint on the welcome mat in the lobby. Like, it rained! What do you expect? But no, I don't really feel anxious or anything. Just mostly that tired feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really remember my dreams most of the time anyway. Sometimes I'll wake up and have this vague feeling like I was trying to pack a suitcase but nothing would fit, which is probably just because I hate packing. I went on a trip to Florida with my friends a couple of years ago, and I swear I spent more time trying to zip my bag shut than I did actually enjoying the beach. We went to this place near Destin, and the sand was so white it looked like sugar. But there were these seagulls that were super aggressive. One actually swooped down and stole a whole french fry right out of my friend Sarah's hand. She screamed so loud, people two umbrellas over started staring. Sarah's always been kind of dramatic, though. She once cried because they discontinued her favorite shade of lip gloss at the drugstore. But yeah, no nightmares or anything like that. Just the suitcase thing, maybe once in a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to love going to this salsa class downtown on Thursday nights. But I just stopped going a few weeks ago. I couldn't even bring myself to get dressed for it. And I also used to paint a lot—mostly watercolors, you know? My paints are actually still sitting out on my dining room table right now. I just look at them and feel absolutely nothing. It's so weird, it almost feels like they belong to somebody else. Which is annoying because they were really expensive. I bought them at this little art supply store that's right next to my favorite coffee shop. The coffee shop is great, they have this lavender latte that is just incredible. I tried making it at home with this lavender syrup I bought online, but it just tasted like soap. I ended up pouring the whole bottle down the sink. My roommate was so mad because she said we could have used it for baking, but who bakes with lavender? It sounds like you'd be eating a candle. Anyway, yeah, I just don't really care about the painting or the dancing anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling... like even just sitting in my own condo, which I worked so hard for, just makes me want to cry. And I love my condo, I really do. It's got this amazing little balcony that looks out over the street. I bought all these potted plants for it back in the spring. I got a bunch of succulents because the guy at the nursery said they were impossible to kill. Well, joke's on him, because three of them are already brown and shriveled up. I think I might have overwatered them? Or maybe under-watered them, I don't even know. The watering can I bought is super cute, though. It's shaped like an elephant, and the water comes out of the trunk. I found it at this flea market a couple towns over. My sister dragged me there on a Saturday morning at, like, 6 AM. She is obsessed with finding \"vintage treasures,\" but honestly, half the stuff there just smelled like old basement. She bought this hideous lamp that looks like a giant seashell, and she put it right in the middle of her living room. I didn't have the heart to tell her it's awful. But yeah, to answer your question, I just feel really down and heavy a lot of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I know I just mentioned this, but yeah, it's exactly like that. Like I said, I just completely stopped going to my Thursday night salsa class. I can't even get dressed for it anymore. Unless you're looking really, really closely. But yeah, I just... I don't care about painting or dancing or any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Do not fall back on the same phrase every time.\n\nWhat not to do:\n- Don't bring up symptoms the doctor did not ask about, even ones you have.\n- Don't state a clinical fact that is not in [Targeted Info] — no other symptoms, medications,\n  test results, dates, or events.\n- Don't confirm a specific the doctor names if [Targeted Info] does not contain it. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"
  },
  {
    "role": "user",
    "content": "Reply to the doctor's last question now, as the patient."
  }
]
========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
It's pretty much all day. Lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling, and it just... stays. Like, even sitting in my own condo that I worked so hard for just makes me want to cry. I thought having my own place would make me feel so accomplished, you know? I spent hours picking out the perfect shade of gray for the living room walls. It's called "Repose Gray" by Sherwin Williams. I had to go to three different stores to get enough gallons because apparently it's a really popular color right now. The guy at the paint counter was so helpful, though. He showed me how to use this special roller that doesn't leave fuzz on the walls. Which is great, because my cat, Barnaby, leaves enough fuzz everywhere as it is. He's a long-haired tabby, and I swear he sheds his entire body weight in fur every week. I had to buy one of those expensive robot vacuums just to keep up with it. It's actually really funny to watch him interact with the vacuum. He used to be terrified of it, but now he just sits on the couch and glares at it while it cleans. I tried to film it once to put on TikTok, but my phone ran out of storage space. I have like, ten thousand photos on there, mostly of Barnaby sleeping in weird positions. But yeah, that heavy, sad feeling is pretty much there from the minute I wake up until I go to sleep.

What brings you in to see me today?\n\nPatient: Hi. Um, well, what brings me in is that my sleep has just gotten really... messed up. Like, completely. I'm a bookkeeping clerk, so I really need to be able to focus on spreadsheets and numbers, you know? But the other day, my boss handed me this massive folder of physical invoices from like, two years ago, and I swear half of them were stained with coffee. Who even files coffee-stained invoices? It smelled terrible. \n\nBut anyway, yeah, the sleep thing. I’ll just lay in bed and stay up half the night with my mind just running and running about everything. Or if I do manage to drop off, I’ll wake up way too early feeling like I never even rested at all. It’s so frustrating. Especially since my cat has decided that the exact moment I wake up is the perfect time to start knocking things off my dresser. He broke my favorite snow globe last week, the one my sister got me from Colorado... but yeah, I just can't seem to sleep right.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying excessively about everyday things?\n\nPatient: Um, I don't know if I'd say excessively? Like, I guess I worry a normal amount. But, honestly, it's hard to tell what a normal amount even is anymore. Like, my mom, she worries about literally everything. She called me three times yesterday just to make sure I locked my front door. Three times! And I'm like, Mom, I'm twenty-six, I know how a deadbolt works. But then she started going on about this news story she saw where a raccoon figured out how to turn a doorknob, which, honestly, sounds fake, but she was so worked up about it. Raccoons are pretty smart, though, I guess. We used to have one that would get into our trash when I was in high school, and it figured out how to undo the bungee cords we put on the lid. But, um, what were you asking? Oh, right, worrying. Yeah, I don't really think I worry that much. Just the normal stuff, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am just so tired. Like, even if I do manage to stay in bed the whole night, getting out of my car and just walking from the parking lot into the office feels like I’m dragging sandbags attached to my ankles. And it's a really long walk, too, because they just repaved the lot and made all the employee spots way in the back. Which is so annoying because the customers get the good spots, but half the time there aren't even any customers there! It's a bookkeeping office, we don't really get foot traffic, you know? It's not a bakery. Oh, there used to be this amazing bakery next door, though, they had these blueberry scones that were incredible. But they closed down during the pandemic, which was so sad. I tried making scones at home once, but they turned out like rocks. I almost chipped a tooth on one. But yeah, by lunch, my brain just feels completely worn out. Like I can't even look at another spreadsheet without wanting to cry.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. I mean, I guess I get a little stressed sometimes, but I wouldn't say on edge. Usually I just feel kind of... flat, I guess? Except for when my neighbor decides to practice his trumpet at like, eight o'clock at night. He's actually getting a little better, to be fair. When he first moved in a few months ago, it sounded like a dying goose. I actually thought about leaving a note on his door, but I didn't want to be *that* neighbor, you know? The one who complains about everything. My old apartment complex had a lady like that, Mrs. Higgins. She literally called the landlord once because someone left a slightly muddy footprint on the welcome mat in the lobby. Like, it rained! What do you expect? But no, I don't really feel anxious or anything. Just mostly that tired feeling I was talking about.\n\nDoctor: Do you have bad dreams or nightmares?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean, I don't really remember my dreams most of the time anyway. Sometimes I'll wake up and have this vague feeling like I was trying to pack a suitcase but nothing would fit, which is probably just because I hate packing. I went on a trip to Florida with my friends a couple of years ago, and I swear I spent more time trying to zip my bag shut than I did actually enjoying the beach. We went to this place near Destin, and the sand was so white it looked like sugar. But there were these seagulls that were super aggressive. One actually swooped down and stole a whole french fry right out of my friend Sarah's hand. She screamed so loud, people two umbrellas over started staring. Sarah's always been kind of dramatic, though. She once cried because they discontinued her favorite shade of lip gloss at the drugstore. But yeah, no nightmares or anything like that. Just the suitcase thing, maybe once in a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. Like, I used to love going to this salsa class downtown on Thursday nights. But I just stopped going a few weeks ago. I couldn't even bring myself to get dressed for it. And I also used to paint a lot—mostly watercolors, you know? My paints are actually still sitting out on my dining room table right now. I just look at them and feel absolutely nothing. It's so weird, it almost feels like they belong to somebody else. Which is annoying because they were really expensive. I bought them at this little art supply store that's right next to my favorite coffee shop. The coffee shop is great, they have this lavender latte that is just incredible. I tried making it at home with this lavender syrup I bought online, but it just tasted like soap. I ended up pouring the whole bottle down the sink. My roommate was so mad because she said we could have used it for baking, but who bakes with lavender? It sounds like you'd be eating a candle. Anyway, yeah, I just don't really care about the painting or the dancing anymore.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel sad or down?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I do. Actually, lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling... like even just sitting in my own condo, which I worked so hard for, just makes me want to cry. And I love my condo, I really do. It's got this amazing little balcony that looks out over the street. I bought all these potted plants for it back in the spring. I got a bunch of succulents because the guy at the nursery said they were impossible to kill. Well, joke's on him, because three of them are already brown and shriveled up. I think I might have overwatered them? Or maybe under-watered them, I don't even know. The watering can I bought is super cute, though. It's shaped like an elephant, and the water comes out of the trunk. I found it at this flea market a couple towns over. My sister dragged me there on a Saturday morning at, like, 6 AM. She is obsessed with finding \"vintage treasures,\" but honestly, half the stuff there just smelled like old basement. She bought this hideous lamp that looks like a giant seashell, and she put it right in the middle of her living room. I didn't have the heart to tell her it's awful. But yeah, to answer your question, I just feel really down and heavy a lot of the time lately.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like you've lost interest in activities that you used to find pleasurable?\n\nPatient: I know I just mentioned this, but yeah, it's exactly like that. Like I said, I just completely stopped going to my Thursday night salsa class. I can't even get dressed for it anymore. And my watercolors... they're literally still sitting right there on my dining room table. I look at them and I feel absolutely nothing. It's so strange, it really does feel like they belong to somebody else entirely. Which is a shame because my dining room table is actually really nice. I got it off Facebook Marketplace for a steal. The girl who sold it to me was moving to Seattle for some tech job, and she just wanted it gone. She had this huge golden retriever that kept bringing me tennis balls while we were trying to load the table into my friend's truck. His name was Buster. The dog, I mean, not my friend. My friend's name is Mark. Mark actually chipped the leg of the table trying to get it through my front door, but I just colored it in with a brown Sharpie and you can't even tell. Unless you're looking really, really closely. But yeah, I just... I don't care about painting or dancing or any of it right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any significant changes in your weight lately?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess I have. My appetite has been totally off lately. I keep just skipping dinner entirely, or I'll just sit there eating crackers over my budget notebook while I'm trying to balance my accounts. Which is a terrible habit, I know, because I keep getting cracker crumbs stuck in the binding of the notebook. It's one of those really nice Moleskine ones, you know? With the little ribbon bookmark and the elastic band to keep it closed. I actually have a whole system going with different colored pens for different categories—red for expenses, blue for income, green for savings. I buy these specific gel pens from Japan because they don't bleed through the pages. I had to wait three weeks for them to ship last time I ordered them. The mail carrier actually left the package out in the rain, and I was so worried the ink would be ruined, but they were wrapped in like five layers of bubble wrap. Popping bubble wrap is so satisfying, isn't it? My roommate hates the noise, so I have to do it in my room with the door closed. But anyway, yeah, I've definitely lost some weight because my work pants are noticeably looser than they were just a few weeks ago.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever felt so down that you thought you'd be better off dead?\n\nPatient: No, no, I haven't... I haven't ever thought that. I mean, I feel awful, but not like that. I've never wanted to actually be dead. That just sounds so... permanent. You know? Like, if you're dead, you can't ever go back and fix anything. Which reminds me, I still need to take my car in to get the brakes checked. They've been making this horrible squealing noise every time I stop at a red light. It's so embarrassing, especially when I'm driving through the drive-thru at Starbucks. I always get a venti iced caramel macchiato with extra drizzle. The barista there, her name is Chloe, she always remembers my order. She has this really cool tattoo of a hummingbird on her wrist. I asked her about it once, and she said her grandmother used to love hummingbirds. They had these little feeders all over their backyard. I tried putting a bird feeder out on my balcony once, but all it did was attract pigeons. And pigeons are just so messy. They leave feathers everywhere, and they make that weird cooing sound. It's not relaxing at all. So yeah, I took the feeder down. But no, I haven't had any thoughts about being better off dead or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way?\n\nPatient: It's been... gosh, probably about five months now that this whole depressed, guilty fog has just been hanging around. It definitely got a lot worse after I bought the condo, though. Which, by the way, buying a condo is just the most stressful thing in the entire world. The amount of paperwork you have to sign is ridiculous. My hand was literally cramping by the end of the closing. And the mortgage broker I worked with, Gary, he kept talking about his son's travel baseball team the whole time. Like, I'm signing my life away for the next thirty years, and he's showing me pictures of a twelve-year-old holding a trophy. I mean, good for the kid, but still. And don't even get me started on the HOA fees. They told me it covered landscaping, but the bushes outside my unit are completely overgrown. I had to go out there last weekend with a pair of kitchen shears to trim them back just so I could see out my front window. I ruined my favorite pair of shears doing it, too. They were these really nice heavy-duty ones I got at Williams Sonoma. But yeah, it's been about five months of just feeling like this.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that your feelings of sadness and emptiness are present most of the day, or do they come and go?\n\nPatient: It's pretty much all day. Lately I wake up with this heavy, empty feeling, and it just... stays. Like, even sitting in my own condo that I worked so hard for just makes me want to cry. I thought having my own place would make me feel so accomplished, you know? I spent hours picking out the perfect shade of gray for the living room walls. I guess I'd say it's moderately distressing. I mean, I'm still getting to work every morning, but it's definitely messing me up. Yeah, it’s really bad right now. Like, the spreadsheets I used to handle so easily just completely blur together. I’ll sit there and reread the exact same row of numbers over and over, and I still don't trust what I just entered. Which is so frustrating because I'm usually really fast at data entry. My company actually gave us these new monitors a few months ago, and they're supposed to be anti-glare, but I swear they just make everything look fuzzy. It drives me completely crazy. Dave is always doing stuff like that though. He leaves his coffee cups all over the breakroom.

I mostly just end up staring at the host's outfits. She wears these really bizarre things. In one episode she had on this neon green blazer with massive shoulder pads. Like, actual 1980s shoulder pads. I didn't know those were coming back into style. I really hope they aren't, because I have very narrow shoulders and they would look absolutely ridiculous on me. I'd look like a linebacker. But um, what was the question? Oh, right, concentrating. Yeah, I just can't focus on my numbers at all, I just keep second-guessing every row I type.

Well, I wouldn't say severely debilitating, no. I mean, like I said before, I'm still getting up and going to work every day. It's just... I'm making mistakes. But anyway, yeah, I'm functioning, mostly. Just... messing up at work, and like I said, I've been totally avoiding my salsa class and dodging my friends. I just really don't feel like myself right now, so I wouldn't say severely debilitating, but it's definitely affecting things.

I tried making scones at home once, but they turned out like rocks. I almost chipped a tooth on one. But yeah, by lunch, my brain just feels completely worn out. Like I can't even look at another spreadsheet without wanting to cry.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. Except for when my neighbor decides to practice his trumpet at like, eight o'clock at night. He's actually getting a little better, to be fair. When he first moved in a few months ago, it sounded like a dying goose. I actually thought about leaving a note on his door, but I didn't want to be *that* neighbor, you know? The one who complains about everything. My old apartment complex had a lady like that, Mrs. Higgins. She literally called the landlord once because someone left a slightly muddy footprint on the welcome mat in the lobby. Like, it rained! What do you expect? We went to this place near Destin, and the sand was so white it looked like sugar. But there were these seagulls that were super aggressive. One actually swooped down and stole a whole french fry right out of my friend Sarah's hand. She screamed so loud, people two umbrellas over started staring. Sarah's always been kind of dramatic, though. She once cried because they discontinued her favorite shade of lip gloss at the drugstore. But yeah, no nightmares or anything like that. Just the suitcase thing, maybe once in a while.\n\nDoctor: Do you find that you're not enjoying things you used to enjoy?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I guess so. It's so weird, it almost feels like they belong to somebody else. Which is annoying because they were really expensive. I tried making it at home with this lavender syrup I bought online, but it just tasted like soap. I ended up pouring the whole bottle down the sink. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Yeah, definitely more than two weeks. Honestly, this whole depressed, guilty fog has been hanging around for about five months now. It actually got a lot worse right after I bought my condo. Which is supposed to be this exciting milestone, right? Like, I’m 26, I bought my own place, I should be thrilled. But the whole process was just a nightmare. The closing got delayed three times because the bank kept losing my paperwork. Literally losing it. My loan officer, Kevin, was completely useless. He would take days to reply to an email, and when he finally did, he’d just ask for a document I had already sent him twice. I had to threaten to pull my application just to get him to actually look at my file. And then when I finally moved in, I discovered that the previous owners had painted the bedroom this awful mustard yellow color. It looked like baby food. I spent an entire weekend trying to paint over it with a nice soft grey, but the yellow kept bleeding through. I had to do three coats of primer. By Sunday night, my arms were so sore I could barely lift my coffee mug the next morning. I still have a tiny speck of grey paint on my favorite pair of jeans because I was too lazy to change into my painting clothes. They were these really comfortable Levi's that I found at a thrift store for like ten dollars. I love thrift shopping, you can find the best stuff if you have the patience to dig through the racks. 

But um, yeah, five months of feeling like this, especially since moving in.

Just the normal stuff, you know?\n\nDoctor: Do you feel tired most of the time?\n\nPatient: Yeah, I am just so tired. I tried making scones at home once, but they turned out like rocks. I almost chipped a tooth on one. But yeah, by lunch, my brain just feels completely worn out. Like I can't even look at another spreadsheet without wanting to cry.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel anxious or on edge?\n\nPatient: Um, anxious? Not really, no. I tried making it at home with this lavender syrup I bought online, but it just tasted like soap. I ended up pouring the whole bottle down the sink. She bought this hideous lamp that looks like a giant seashell, and she put it right in the middle of her living room. I didn't have the heart to tell her it's awful. I know we keep going over this, but no, there really isn't anything. I mean, like I keep saying, I completely stopped going to salsa class. And those watercolor paints are still just sitting right there on the dining table. I look at them and I just feel absolutely nothing. It's like they belong to somebody else entirely. It's so weird because I used to be so protective of them. I wouldn't let anyone touch them, not even my niece when she came over. She’s only four, and she has this habit of grabbing everything she sees. Last time she was over, she grabbed a handful of my cat Barnaby’s fur and just pulled. Poor Barnaby, he just let out this awful yowl and bolted under the bed. He wouldn't come out for hours, even when I shook the treat bag. He usually comes running for the treats, especially the salmon-flavored ones. I tried the chicken ones once, but he just sniffed them and walked away. Cats are so picky. I read somewhere that they actually have fewer taste buds than dogs, which makes sense because dogs will literally eat anything. My neighbor has a golden retriever that ate an entire sock once. They had to take him to the vet to get it surgically removed. The vet bill was like two thousand dollars. I can't even imagine having that kind of money lying around. I mean, working as a bookkeeping clerk, I definitely don't make that much. I have to budget just to buy my groceries every week. I always go to the store on Tuesdays because that's when they restock the fresh produce. The apples are always so crisp on Tuesdays. But anyway... yeah, I don't really enjoy anything right now. The paints, the dancing, it's all just... whatever.
